    p='app/static/app.js'; s=open(p).read()
    start = s.index('/* ---------- bottom statistics (static, test period) ---------- */')
    end = s.index('/* ---------- orchestration ---------- */')
    new_bottom = r'''/* ---------- bottom row: Today / Last 7 days / Model ---------- */
    const tile = (k, v, d = "", cls = "") => `<div class="kpi"><span class="k">${k}</span><span class="v">${v}</span><span class="d ${cls}">${d}</span></div>`;
    const cards = () => [1, 2, 3].map((i) => ({ head: $(`c${i}-head`), el: $(`c${i}`) }));
    const FEATURE_NAMES = { region: "Oblast", hour_kyiv: "Hour of day", dow_kyiv: "Day of week", min_since_last_end: "Time since last alert",
      starts_24h: "Alerts last 24 h", starts_7d: "Alerts last 7 d", n_active_oblasts: "Oblasts on alert now", national_starts_1h: "National starts 1 h",
      nb_active_frac: "Neighbours on alert", nb_starts_1h: "Neighbour starts 1 h" };
    
    function setView(v) {
      state.view = v;
      document.querySelectorAll("#bottom-tabs .seg").forEach((b) => b.classList.toggle("active", b.dataset.view === v));
      renderBottom();
    }
    
    let bottomSeq = 0;
    async function renderBottom() {
      const seq = ++bottomSeq;
      if (state.view === "model") return renderModelView();
      if (!state.snap) return;
      const live = state.mode === "now";
      const period = state.view === "today" ? "today" : "week";
      let g;
      try {
        g = await getJSON(`/api/glance?t=${encodeURIComponent(state.snap.t)}&period=${period}&live=${live}`);
      } catch (e) { $("glance-note").innerHTML = `<span class="warn">${e.message}</span>`; return; }
      if (seq !== bottomSeq) return; // a newer request (time step / tab switch) superseded this one
      renderGlance(g);
    }
    
    function renderGlance(g) {
      const [c1, c2, c3] = cards();
      const span = g.period === "today" ? `since 00:00 Kyiv` : `${fmtShort.format(new Date(g.start))} – ${fmtShort.format(new Date(g.end))}`;
      $("glance-note").innerHTML = span + (g.data_until ? ` · <span class="warn">history only until ${fmtKyiv.format(new Date(g.data_until))}</span>` : "");
      const L = g.longest, P = g.peak;
      $("kpis").innerHTML =
        tile("Alerts started", g.alerts_started, g.period === "today" ? "today" : "last 7 days") +
        tile("Alert-hours", `${g.alert_hours}h`, "summed over oblasts") +
        tile("Oblasts affected", `${g.oblasts_affected}/${g.oblasts_total}`, "had at least one alert") +
        tile("Median duration", g.median_duration_min == null ? "—" : mins(g.median_duration_min), "finished alerts") +
        tile("Longest alert", L ? mins(L.minutes) : "—", L ? `${label(L.region)}${L.ongoing ? " · ongoing" : ""}` : "") +
        tile("Peak at once", P ? `${P.oblasts} obl.` : "—", P ? fmtKyiv.format(new Date(P.t)) : "");
    
      c1.head.innerHTML = `Most affected oblasts <span class="muted">alert-hours</span>`;
      drawBars(c1.el, g.by_region.slice(0, 8).map((d) => ({ k: d.label, v: d.hours, tip: `<b>${d.label}</b><br>${d.hours.toFixed(1)} alert-hours · ${d.alerts} alerts started` })),
        (v) => `${v.toFixed(1)}h`, 90);
    
      c2.head.innerHTML = `Oblasts under alert <span class="muted">${g.period === "today" ? "every 10 min" : "hourly"}</span>`;
      drawTimeline(c2.el, g.timeline);
    
      c3.head.innerHTML = `How long alerts lasted <span class="muted">finished alerts</span>`;
      drawColumns(c3.el, g.durations);
    }
    
    function renderModelView() {
      const m = state.meta.metrics;
      const [c1, c2, c3] = cards();
      $("glance-note").textContent = `test period ${fmtShort.format(new Date(m.test_start))} – ${fmtShort.format(new Date(m.test_end))}`;
      const rel = (a, b, lowerBetter) => {
        const diff = ((a - b) / b) * 100, better = lowerBetter ? diff < 0 : diff > 0;
        return [`${diff > 0 ? "+" : ""}${diff.toFixed(1)}% vs baseline ${b.toFixed(3)}`, better ? "better" : "worse"];
      };
      const [bd, bc] = rel(m.start_brier, m.start_baseline_brier, true);
      const [ad, ac] = rel(m.start_roc_auc, m.start_baseline_roc_auc, false);
      const [pd_, pc] = rel(m.start_pr_auc, m.start_baseline_pr_auc, false);
      const [md, mc] = rel(m.dur_mae_median_min, m.dur_mae_baseline_min, true);
      $("kpis").innerHTML =
        tile("Start · Brier", m.start_brier.toFixed(3), bd, bc) +
        tile("Start · ROC-AUC", m.start_roc_auc.toFixed(3), ad, ac) +
        tile("Start · PR-AUC", m.start_pr_auc.toFixed(3), pd_, pc) +
        tile("Duration · MAE", `${m.dur_mae_median_min.toFixed(1)}m`, md.replace(/baseline ([\d.]+)/, "baseline $1m"), mc) +
        tile("Duration · 80% cover", pct(m.dur_coverage_80), "target 80%", Math.abs(m.dur_coverage_80 - 0.8) < 0.05 ? "better" : "worse") +
        tile("Duration · C-index", m.dur_c_index.toFixed(3), `${m.dur_test_events} test alerts`);
      c1.head.innerHTML = `Calibration <span class="muted">test period</span>`;
      drawCalibration(c1.el);
      c2.head.innerHTML = `Training curve <span class="muted">log loss per boosting round</span>`;
      drawCurves(c2.el);
      c3.head.innerHTML = `What drives the forecast <span class="muted">LightGBM gain</span>`;
      const imp = Object.entries(state.meta.importance).map(([k, v]) => ({ k: FEATURE_NAMES[k] ?? k, v }));
      const total = d3.sum(imp, (d) => d.v);
      drawBars(c3.el, imp.sort((a, b) => b.v - a.v).map((d) => ({ ...d, tip: `<b>${d.k}</b><br>${pct(d.v / total)} of total gain` })), (v) => pct(v / total), 140);
    }
    
    function drawBars(el, data, fmt, labelWidth) {
      const f = frame(el, { t: 6, r: 50, b: 6, l: labelWidth });
      const y = d3.scaleBand(data.map((d) => d.k), [0, f.ih]).padding(0.3), x = d3.scaleLinear([0, d3.max(data, (d) => d.v) || 1], [0, f.iw]);
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").call(d3.axisLeft(y).tickSize(0).tickPadding(8)).select(".domain").remove();
      f.g.selectAll("rect").data(data).join("rect").attr("y", (d) => y(d.k)).attr("height", y.bandwidth()).attr("width", (d) => Math.max(1, x(d.v)))
        .attr("rx", 2).attr("fill", css("--series-model"))
        .on("mousemove", (ev, d) => showTip(ev, d.tip)).on("mouseleave", hideTip);
      f.g.selectAll("text.v").data(data).join("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("x", (d) => x(d.v) + 4)
        .attr("y", (d) => y(d.k) + y.bandwidth() / 2 + 3).text((d) => fmt(d.v));
    }
    
    function drawColumns(el, data) {
      const f = frame(el, { t: 14, r: 10, b: 20, l: 30 });
      const x = d3.scaleBand(data.map((d) => d.bin), [0, f.iw]).padding(0.25), y = d3.scaleLinear([0, d3.max(data, (d) => d.n) || 1], [f.ih, 0]).nice();
      const total = d3.sum(data, (d) => d.n) || 1;
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").attr("transform", `translate(0,${f.ih})`).call(d3.axisBottom(x).tickSize(0).tickPadding(6));
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").call(d3.axisLeft(y).ticks(3));
      f.g.selectAll("rect").data(data).join("rect").attr("x", (d) => x(d.bin)).attr("width", x.bandwidth())
        .attr("y", (d) => y(d.n)).attr("height", (d) => f.ih - y(d.n)).attr("rx", 2).attr("fill", css("--series-model"))
        .on("mousemove", (ev, d) => showTip(ev, `<b>${d.bin}</b><br>${d.n} alerts · ${pct(d.n / total)}`)).on("mouseleave", hideTip);
      f.g.selectAll("text.v").data(data).join("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("text-anchor", "middle")
        .attr("x", (d) => x(d.bin) + x.bandwidth() / 2).attr("y", (d) => y(d.n) - 3).text((d) => pct(d.n / total));
    }
    
    function drawTimeline(el, tl) {
      const f = frame(el, { t: 10, r: 14, b: 22, l: 30 });
      const ts = tl.t.map((v) => new Date(v));
      if (ts.length < 2) return;
      const x = d3.scaleTime(d3.extent(ts), [0, f.iw]), y = d3.scaleLinear([0, Math.max(4, d3.max(tl.active))], [f.ih, 0]).nice();
      const short = ts.at(-1) - ts[0] <= 864e5;
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").attr("transform", `translate(0,${f.ih})`)
        .call(d3.axisBottom(x).ticks(Math.max(2, Math.floor(f.iw / 80))).tickFormat((v) => (short ? fmtHM : fmtShort).format(v)));
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").call(d3.axisLeft(y).ticks(4));
      const pts = ts.map((d, i) => [d, tl.active[i]]);
      f.g.append("path").datum(pts).attr("fill", css("--critical")).attr("fill-opacity", 0.2).attr("d", d3.area((p) => x(p[0]), f.ih, (p) => y(p[1])).curve(d3.curveStepAfter));
      f.g.append("path").datum(pts).attr("fill", "none").attr("stroke", css("--critical")).attr("stroke-width", 1.5)
        .attr("d", d3.line((p) => x(p[0]), (p) => y(p[1])).curve(d3.curveStepAfter));
      hoverTime(f, x, ts, (i) => `${fmtKyiv.format(ts[i])}<br><b>${tl.active[i]}</b> oblasts under alert`);
    }
    
    function drawCurves(el) {
      const rows = state.meta.curves;
      const f = frame(el, { t: 10, r: 60, b: 22, l: 40 });
      if (!rows?.length) return;
      const best = state.meta.metrics.start_best_rounds;
      const x = d3.scaleLinear([1, rows.length], [0, f.iw]);
      const vals = rows.flatMap((r) => [r.train_binary_logloss, r.valid_binary_logloss]);
      const y = d3.scaleLinear(d3.extent(vals), [f.ih, 0]).nice();
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").attr("transform", `translate(0,${f.ih})`).call(d3.axisBottom(x).ticks(6));
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").call(d3.axisLeft(y).ticks(4).tickFormat(d3.format(".3f")));
      const series = [["train_binary_logloss", "train", css("--series-model")], ["valid_binary_logloss", "validation", css("--series-baseline")]];
      for (const [key, name, color] of series) {
        f.g.append("path").datum(rows).attr("fill", "none").attr("stroke", color).attr("stroke-width", 2).attr("d", d3.line((r) => x(r.round), (r) => y(r[key])));
        f.g.append("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("x", f.iw + 4).attr("y", y(rows.at(-1)[key]) + 3).text(name);
      }
      f.g.append("line").attr("x1", x(best)).attr("x2", x(best)).attr("y1", 0).attr("y2", f.ih).attr("stroke", css("--text-muted")).attr("stroke-dasharray", "3 3");
      f.g.append("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("x", x(best) + 4).attr("y", 10).text(`best: ${best} trees`);
      const rounds = rows.map((r) => r.round);
      hoverTime(f, x, rounds, (i) => `round <b>${rounds[i]}</b><br>train ${rows[i].train_binary_logloss.toFixed(4)}<br>validation ${rows[i].valid_binary_logloss.toFixed(4)}`);
    }
    
    function drawCalibration(el) {
      const f = frame(el, { t: 10, r: 18, b: 32, l: 36 });
      const x = d3.scaleLinear([0, 1], [0, f.iw]), y = d3.scaleLinear([0, 1], [f.ih, 0]);
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").attr("transform", `translate(0,${f.ih})`).call(d3.axisBottom(x).ticks(5).tickFormat(d3.format(".0%")));
      f.g.append("g").attr("class", "axis").call(d3.axisLeft(y).ticks(5).tickFormat(d3.format(".0%")));
      f.g.append("line").attr("x1", 0).attr("y1", f.ih).attr("x2", f.iw).attr("y2", 0).attr("class", "gridline").attr("stroke-dasharray", "3 3");
      f.g.append("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("x", f.iw - 2).attr("y", 12).attr("text-anchor", "end").text("perfect");
      const data = state.meta.calibration.filter((d) => d.n >= 30); // tiny bins (e.g. n=1) are noise
      f.g.append("path").datum(data).attr("fill", "none").attr("stroke", css("--series-model")).attr("stroke-width", 2)
        .attr("d", d3.line((d) => x(d.mean_pred), (d) => y(d.frac_pos)));
      f.g.selectAll("circle").data(data).join("circle").attr("cx", (d) => x(d.mean_pred)).attr("cy", (d) => y(d.frac_pos)).attr("r", 4)
        .attr("fill", css("--series-model")).attr("stroke", css("--panel")).attr("stroke-width", 2)
        .on("mousemove", (ev, d) => showTip(ev, `predicted <b>${pct(d.mean_pred)}</b> → actual <b>${pct(d.frac_pos)}</b><br><span class="mono">${d.n} oblast-hours</span>`))
        .on("mouseleave", hideTip);
      f.svg.append("text").attr("class", "series-label").attr("x", f.m.l + f.iw / 2).attr("y", f.h - 1).attr("text-anchor", "middle").text("predicted probability");
    }

==== CLAUDE (2026-10-12 15:08:35 UTC) ====
Everything you asked for is in: the neighbour feature, proper W&B training curves, and the Today / Last 7 days tabs. Training takes about 20 s and all 4 leakage tests pass. Answers to your questions:

## 1. Is hour of day sine-encoded?

**No, and testing showed it shouldn't be for this model.** In the duration model (a linear model) I *do* use sin/cos for the hour. LightGBM is made of decision trees, and a tree can split "hour < 6" or "hour > 22" directly. It doesn't care that 23 and 0 are far apart as numbers. With sin/cos, the tree needs two features together to describe one hour, which makes splitting harder.

I tested this on the validation period only, so the test set stays untouched:

| Variant | Log loss ↓ | ROC‑AUC ↑ |
|---|---|---|
| raw hour/day | 0.4420 | 0.7743 |
| sin/cos hour/day | 0.4433 | 0.7730 |
| **raw + neighbours** | **0.4385** | **0.7766** |
| sin/cos + neighbours | 0.4389 | 0.7766 |

So the model keeps raw hour/day. Your **neighbour feature helps**, so it's in: the share of neighbouring oblasts on alert now, plus alerts started in neighbours in the last hour.
- **How the neighbours were found:** I computed them from the map shapes instead of writing them from memory, and checked them against real borders.
- **One quirk:** Odesa and Kherson count as neighbours because their shapes touch across the Dnipro–Buh estuary.
- **Honest note:** the improvement on the test set is smaller than on validation. Test Brier is 0.1521, vs 0.1524 before.

## 2. Weather data

It's possible: Open‑Meteo is free with no API key, and gives hourly temperature, wind, low clouds and rain per point. There are two problems:
- **Leakage trap.** The API returned values up to 23:00 tonight, and it's 18:08 now. For recent hours these are **forecasts**, and for older hours they're forecasts saved at the time. That's usable without leakage only if we're careful: at time t, use only weather published before t.
- **Weak link to our target.** Weather affects drones, but the decision to launch an attack doesn't depend on the weather in *this* oblast right now.

I'd test it like the neighbour feature: on validation, keeping it only if it helps. It takes about 30–45 minutes, so do it only if time allows tonight.

## 3. Model 2 in simple words

- **The idea:** alert durations are very uneven. Most are short (median about 15 min), but a few last hours. If you take the **logarithm** of the duration, the shape becomes a normal **bell curve**.
- **What it learns:** where the center of the bell is for this oblast, at this hour, with this many oblasts on alert. That's a linear formula. It also learns one width (σ) for how uncertain it is.
- **What it outputs:** from the bell we get P(still active after t minutes) for any t (the curve in the right panel), the median ("most likely end"), and an 80% range.
- **If 20 minutes have already passed:** we cut off the part of the curve before 20 minutes and rescale the rest.

**Log‑likelihood:** for each real alert in training, ask the model "how probable was the duration that actually happened?", take the log of that probability, and add it up over all alerts. A good model gives high probability to what really happened, so higher is better. Training **maximizes** it (in practice, minimizes its negative).

**BFGS:** the algorithm that finds the best formula. It starts with a guess, computes which direction makes the likelihood better (the gradient), and takes a step. It also remembers previous steps to estimate the curvature of the surface, which lets it take smart, large steps instead of tiny ones. It's standard for small smooth problems like this one: about 30 numbers to find, solved in seconds.

**A model-selection finding:** I compared three bell shapes on validation. **Weibull fits better than log‑normal** (log‑likelihood ‑4.31 vs ‑4.36 per alert). The prediction code currently supports only log‑normal. Switching takes about 20 minutes, and I'd recommend it.

## 4. Model selection: why not an LSTM?

You're right. Here's the case against an LSTM:
1. **Data size.** We have about 180k oblast-hours but only about 33k alerts, a small dataset. LSTMs need much more data to beat simpler models.
2. **Compute.** No GPU, a 4‑core CPU, and a budget of a couple of minutes. An LSTM would need many epochs plus tuning, while LightGBM finds the best tree count in **3 seconds**.
3. **Evidence.** On forecasting tasks with this kind of data, gradient boosting with "lag" features usually wins. The M5 forecasting competition was won by LightGBM. The LSTM's advantage, learning patterns from long raw sequences, matters less when we can compute the useful history directly ("alerts in the last 24 h", "neighbours on alert").

What I'd actually compare:
- **Model 1:**
  - logistic regression, a simple honest baseline
  - LightGBM, the current model
  - a **Hawkes process**, a classic time-series model for events. It assumes "an alert makes more alerts likely soon, here and in neighbouring oblasts", which matches this problem well. It's more work, but interesting to talk about in an interview.
- **Model 2:** log‑normal vs Weibull (already measured), plus LightGBM predicting quantiles.

All of these train in seconds to a minute.

## 5. W&B: fixed

Training now uses three periods: **train → validation (14 days) → test (28 days)**.
- **Curves:** in W&B, open the newest run and look at the `start/` charts: train and validation log loss and AUC for every boosting round. The same curve is in the dashboard's **Model** tab.
- **Number of trees:** the curve shows validation stops improving at **124 trees**, so the old fixed 400 was too many.
- **Final model:** refit on train plus validation with 124 trees.
- **Summary values** (the final scores) are now in the run summary, not shown as single-dot charts.
- **Ablation runs:** the four feature-test runs are in W&B too.

## 6. Do we build our own dataset from live data? Is it stored?

**No.** The live feed is only held in memory for 60 seconds and never saved. Building a recorder (save every on/off change with its time to a file) would:
- give us start times for live alerts, so end-time predictions get better
- fix the stale history in Now mode
- build our own dataset over time

## 7. First-time users: are the weights stored?

**Yes.** The `models/` folder holds only the latest final model, and it's small:
- `start_model.txt`: 428 KB
- `duration_model.json`: 4 KB
- metrics and replay data: about 350 KB

`models/` isn't in `.gitignore`, so it goes to GitHub, and a new user only runs `uv sync` and `uv run python -m app`. No retraining, no W&B key needed. There's one copy (the final refit model), with no snapshots.

**Nothing is committed yet.** That's the next thing to do.

## 8. Today / Last 7 days

The bottom row now has tabs: **Today | Last 7 days | Model**.
- **Statistics:** alerts started, alert-hours, oblasts affected, median duration, longest alert, and the peak number of oblasts on alert at once.
- **Charts:** most affected oblasts, oblasts on alert over time, and how long alerts lasted.
- **In Replay**, everything is computed only from what was known at the selected moment.
- **In Now mode**, it shows a yellow warning that today's history ends at 07:59. That's the same stale-data problem, so the recorder would help here too.

## 9. What I run in the shell

- `uv run …` to train, run tests and run small checks
- `curl` to test APIs (Telegram, ubilling, Open‑Meteo)
- **The dashboard server, in the background on port 8765.** It's still running.
- **Headless browsers for screenshots:** Firefox with a temporary profile, then Playwright with Chromium. Playwright downloaded Chromium (~100 MB) into `~/.cache/ms-playwright`, outside the project. Tell me if you want it deleted.
- Small Python scripts that edit files. They're harder to read in the log than the normal edit tool, so I'll use the edit tool more from now on.
- **One mistake:** `pkill` killed its own shell once. Now I stop processes by exact PID.
- **Also found:** your own Firefox (open for 5 hours) was using **87% CPU** with less than 1 GB of RAM free. That made one experiment take 8 minutes instead of 1, so I limited LightGBM to 4 threads.
